# Daily Summary: 2025-09-15

### OmniEVA: Embodied Versatile Planner via Task-Adaptive 3D-Grounded and Embodiment-aware Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09332v2)
- **Authors**: Yuecheng Liu, Dafeng Chi, Shiguang Wu, Zhanguang Zhang, Yuzheng Zhuang, Bowen Yang, He Zhu, Lingfeng Zhang, Pengwei Xie, David Gamaliel Arcos Bravo, Yingxue Zhang, Jianye Hao, Xingyue Quan
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have opened new opportunities for embodied intelligence, enabling multimodal understanding, reasoning, and interaction, as well as continuous spatial decision-making. Nevertheless, current MLLM-based embodied systems face two critical limitations. First, Geometric Adaptability Gap: models trained solely on 2D inputs or with hard-coded 3D geometry injection suffer from either insufficient spatial information or restricted 2D generalization, leading to poor adaptability across tasks with diverse spatial demands. Second, Embodiment Constraint Gap: prior work often neglects the physical constraints and capacities of real robots, resulting in task plans that are theoretically valid but practically infeasible. To address these gaps, we introduce OmniEVA -- an embodied versatile planner that enables advanced embodied reasoning and task planning through two pivotal innovations: (1) a Task-Adaptive 3D Grounding mechanism, which introduces a gated router to perform explicit selective regulation of 3D fusion based on contextual requirements, enabling context-aware 3D grounding for diverse embodied tasks. (2) an Embodiment-Aware Reasoning framework that jointly incorporates task goals and embodiment constraints into the reasoning loop, resulting in planning decisions that are both goal-directed and executable. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that OmniEVA not only achieves state-of-the-art general embodied reasoning performance, but also exhibits a strong ability across a wide range of downstream scenarios. Evaluations of a suite of proposed embodied benchmarks, including both primitive and composite tasks, confirm its robust and versatile planning capabilities. Project page: https://omnieva.github.io
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "OMNIEVA: EMBODIED VERSATILE PLANNER VIA TASK-ADAPTIVE 3D-GROUNDED AND EMBODIMENT-AWARE REASONING":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OmniEVA, a novel embodied versatile planner designed to address two key limitations in existing MLLM-based embodied systems: (1) the "Geometric Adaptability Gap," where models struggle with tasks requiring strong spatial reasoning due to insufficient 3D information or restricted 2D generalization; and (2) the "Embodiment Constraint Gap," where models neglect the physical constraints and capabilities of real robots, leading to infeasible plans. OmniEVA innovates with a "Task-Adaptive 3D Grounding" mechanism, using a gated router to selectively regulate 3D feature fusion based on contextual task requirements. It also employs an "Embodiment-Aware Reasoning" framework that incorporates task goals and embodiment constraints into the reasoning loop, resulting in more executable plans. The authors demonstrate OmniEVA's effectiveness through extensive experiments on 8 established benchmarks, showing state-of-the-art performance and versatility across a wide range of tasks.  They introduce new embodied benchmarks and show superior performance on primitive tasks, highlighting OmniEVA's ability to plan and execute in embodied environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main contributions lie in the Task-Adaptive 3D Grounding (TAGR) and Embodiment-Aware Reasoning (TE-GRPO) mechanisms. The dynamic fusion of 3D information based on task requirements is a valuable advancement. The TE-GRPO is also compelling, addressing the crucial gap between theoretical planning and real-world robotic feasibility. While some prior work touches on 3D reasoning and embodiment, the combination of adaptive 3D grounding with a specific focus on *executable* plans differentiates OmniEVA. The introduction of new primitive embodied benchmarks to assess specific capabilities is also a positive contribution.

*   **Significance:** The limitations identified (geometric adaptability and embodiment constraint gaps) are indeed critical challenges for embodied AI. OmniEVA's ability to perform well on both 2D and 3D reasoning tasks, alongside its improved real-world executability, demonstrates its potential to advance the field. The thorough evaluation across various benchmarks strengthens the paper's claims. The project page and any released code/models will further increase the paper's impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem statement and clear articulation of the gaps in current approaches.
    *   Technically sound and well-explained architecture and training methodology.
    *   Extensive experimental validation across a diverse set of benchmarks.
    *   Introduction of new, targeted embodied benchmarks.
    *   Demonstrated real-world improvements in task execution.
    *   Improved SOTA in multiple datasets.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While TAGR achieves some improvements by dynamically selecting 3D features, the improvements aren't *huge*. It would be interesting to deeply dive into the failure cases of TAGR (perhaps the router is imperfectly tuned and occasionally turns off 3D when it shouldn't).
    *   The improvements in real-world robotic execution depend on the fidelity of the simulator and the low-level control policies. The paper acknowledges the latter as a potential bottleneck, but more discussion on the simulator setup would be beneficial.
    *   Ablation analysis reveals significant individual contribution of the Task reward and the Embodiment reward. While it's positive that the combination yields the best result, understanding their interaction and relative importance would be better.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has good potential to influence the embodied AI field. The emphasis on adaptability and executability is timely and relevant. Other researchers could build on OmniEVA's architecture and training strategies, particularly the TE-GRPO framework. The new benchmarks could become valuable tools for evaluating future embodied systems.

*   **Room for Improvement:** Quantitatively evaluate TAGR's failure cases/what it doesn't help with, include more simulator details and compare to state-of-the-art methods without large language models.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** OmniEVA presents a notable advancement in embodied AI by addressing critical limitations and introducing novel mechanisms for adaptive 3D grounding and embodiment-aware reasoning. The thorough experimental validation and potential for real-world impact justify a high score. While there are areas for further investigation (TAGR's failure cases, simulator fidelity), the paper makes a significant contribution to the field and has good potential for future influence.
- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 8/10

### MetaRAG: Metamorphic Testing for Hallucination Detection in RAG Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09360v1)
- **Authors**: Channdeth Sok, David Luz, Yacine Haddam
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in enterprise applications, yet their reliability remains limited by hallucinations, i.e., confident but factually incorrect information. Existing detection approaches, such as SelfCheckGPT and MetaQA, primarily target standalone LLMs and do not address the unique challenges of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems, where responses must be consistent with retrieved evidence. We therefore present MetaRAG, a metamorphic testing framework for hallucination detection in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. MetaRAG operates in a real-time, unsupervised, black-box setting, requiring neither ground-truth references nor access to model internals, making it suitable for proprietary and high-stakes domains. The framework proceeds in four stages: (1) decompose answers into atomic factoids, (2) generate controlled mutations of each factoid using synonym and antonym substitutions, (3) verify each variant against the retrieved context (synonyms are expected to be entailed and antonyms contradicted), and (4) aggregate penalties for inconsistencies into a response-level hallucination score. Crucially for identity-aware AI, MetaRAG localizes unsupported claims at the factoid span where they occur (e.g., pregnancy-specific precautions, LGBTQ+ refugee rights, or labor eligibility), allowing users to see flagged spans and enabling system designers to configure thresholds and guardrails for identity-sensitive queries. Experiments on a proprietary enterprise dataset illustrate the effectiveness of MetaRAG for detecting hallucinations and enabling trustworthy deployment of RAG-based conversational agents. We also outline a topic-based deployment design that translates MetaRAG's span-level scores into identity-aware safeguards; this design is discussed but not evaluated in our experiments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MetaRAG: Metamorphic Testing for Hallucination Detection in RAG Systems":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MetaRAG, a novel metamorphic testing framework designed to detect hallucinations (factually incorrect information) in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. Unlike existing hallucination detection methods that often require gold-standard references or access to model internals, MetaRAG operates in a black-box, zero-resource setting, making it suitable for proprietary environments. MetaRAG decomposes LLM responses into factoids, applies controlled mutations (synonym and antonym substitutions), and verifies the mutated factoids against the retrieved context to identify inconsistencies. The framework generates a response-level hallucination score and, importantly, identifies unsupported spans at the factoid level, facilitating identity-aware safeguards. The authors evaluate MetaRAG on a proprietary enterprise dataset and demonstrate its effectiveness in detecting hallucinations, and showcase potential applications for identity-aware safeguards through case studies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in adapting metamorphic testing specifically for hallucination detection in RAG systems operating in a black-box setting. Existing metamorphic testing (MT) work, such as MetaQA and MORTAR, primarily targeted open-book question answering or multi-turn dialogue. The adaptation of MT for RAG, particularly in the context of proprietary data where reference answers are unavailable, is a valuable innovation. Additionally, the factoid-level decomposition and targeted mutations (synonyms and antonyms) are well-suited to identifying factual inconsistencies in RAG outputs, and the potential for identity-aware integration is a strong direction.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in deploying LLMs in enterprise settings: the risk of hallucinations. The ability to detect and flag factually incorrect information, especially in sensitive domains like healthcare and law, is crucial for building trustworthy AI systems. MetaRAG's black-box nature is a significant advantage, as it enables deployment with proprietary models and data where internal access is restricted. The focus on identity-aware safeguards, though not fully evaluated empirically, offers a promising approach to mitigate disproportionate harm to specific populations. The method itself appears to be fairly computationally intensive for large-scale applications, which is noted by the authors. This poses a practical problem and limits its scalability.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical applicability:** The black-box and zero-resource design makes MetaRAG highly practical for real-world deployments in enterprise environments.
    *   **Factoid-level localization:** Identifying unsupported spans at the factoid level provides actionable insights for improving response quality and enabling targeted interventions.
    *   **Identity-aware potential:** The paper proposes a valuable approach to connect hallucination detection to fairness and mitigate biases by allowing topic-conditioned safeguards.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The ablation study is thorough and provides insights into the contribution of individual components.
    *   **Clarity of Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and structured, making it easy to follow the proposed method and understand the experiments.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited empirical evaluation of identity-aware safeguards:** The paper outlines potential identity-aware safeguards but does not empirically evaluate their effectiveness in mitigating bias or harm. This needs to be addressed in further studies. The case studies are helpful, but not enough to support the claim of identity-aware safeguards.
    *   **Dataset:** Although use of a proprietary dataset is realistic and strengthens a claim of applicability to real-world scenarios, its nature makes it difficult for researchers to fully examine or use as a benchmark. The inability to analyze the types of errors made by MetaRAG further limits the evaluation.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The multiple LLM calls required for factoid decomposition, mutation generation, and verification could make MetaRAG computationally expensive, particularly for large-scale applications or real-time systems. This is a key practical limitation.
    *   **Model dependency:** The results are dependent on specific LLMs, specifically GPT-4.1 variants. The authors note that they did not include Claude Sonnet in the verification or zero-shot experiments.
    * **Hallucination Definition:** The paper, like many in the field, struggles with a consistent and actionable definition of "hallucination". The use of semantic entailment and contradiction provides an explicit criterion, but it may miss instances where the LLM's response deviates from the reference in ways that are technically faithful but nonetheless misleading or unhelpful.

*   **Potential Influence:** MetaRAG has the potential to influence the development of more robust and trustworthy RAG systems. Its black-box nature could promote wider adoption of hallucination detection methods in enterprise settings. The focus on factoid-level localization and identity-aware safeguards may inspire further research on fairness and bias mitigation in LLMs.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and practical approach to hallucination detection in RAG systems, addressing a crucial challenge in deploying LLMs in real-world environments. The black-box nature, factoid-level analysis, and identity-aware integration potential are valuable contributions. However, the limited empirical evaluation of identity-aware safeguards, a key proposed benefit of their work, and high computational cost constrain the impact and practical utility of the current work. The heavy reliance on closed-source, inaccessible LLMs limits its accessibility and reproducibility. Further research is needed to address these weaknesses and fully realize the potential of MetaRAG.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Plug-and-play Diffusion Models for Image Compressive Sensing with Data Consistency Projection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09365v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaodong Wang, Ping Wang, Zhangyuan Li, Xin Yuan
- **Abstract**: We explore the connection between Plug-and-Play (PnP) methods and Denoising Diffusion Implicit Models (DDIM) for solving ill-posed inverse problems, with a focus on single-pixel imaging. We begin by identifying key distinctions between PnP and diffusion models-particularly in their denoising mechanisms and sampling procedures. By decoupling the diffusion process into three interpretable stages: denoising, data consistency enforcement, and sampling, we provide a unified framework that integrates learned priors with physical forward models in a principled manner. Building upon this insight, we propose a hybrid data-consistency module that linearly combines multiple PnP-style fidelity terms. This hybrid correction is applied directly to the denoised estimate, improving measurement consistency without disrupting the diffusion sampling trajectory. Experimental results on single-pixel imaging tasks demonstrate that our method achieves better reconstruction quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel approach for image compressive sensing (specifically single-pixel imaging) by integrating Plug-and-Play (PnP) optimization techniques with Denoising Diffusion Implicit Models (DDIMs).  The core idea is to decouple the DDIM sampling process into three stages: denoising, data consistency enforcement, and sampling. The authors then replace the data consistency step in DDIM with a PnP-style optimization module. Furthermore, they introduce a *hybrid* data consistency update that linearly combines different PnP-style fidelity terms (specifically from PnP-GAP and PnP-HQS) to better balance measurement and prior fidelity.  Experimental results on single-pixel imaging tasks demonstrate that the proposed method achieves better reconstruction quality compared to baseline DDIM approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of this paper lies in several key aspects:

    *   **Explicit Decoupling and Reintegration:** The explicit breakdown of the DDIM sampling process into denoising, data consistency, and sampling provides a clearer framework for understanding and modifying diffusion models for inverse problems. This is not entirely new, as others have considered similar decompositions, but the explicit connection to the PnP framework and use of hybrid data consistency makes it a valuable contribution.
    *   **Hybrid Data Consistency:**  The introduction of a hybrid data-consistency module, combining PnP-GAP and PnP-HQS updates, is a novel way to improve measurement consistency while maintaining robustness to noise. This combines a hard constraint and a least-squares objective together, leading to an interesting synergy. This is, arguably, the most significant innovation.
    *   **Fusion of Physical Constraints and Generative Priors:** While the broad idea of combining physical models and diffusion priors is well-established, the proposed method provides a specific, principled way to do so, grounded in PnP optimization.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms baseline DDIM approaches on single-pixel imaging tasks. This is a concrete indication of the method's potential for practical applications in scenarios where compressive sensing is crucial.
    *   **Theoretical Grounding:** Linking diffusion models with PnP provides a strong theoretical base for combining generative priors with physical measurement models. The PnP framework is well-understood and the theoretical properties can be leveraged to improve the performance and robustness of diffusion models for inverse problems. This facilitates analyzing convergence and guarantees.
    *   **Generalizability:** While the experiments are focused on single-pixel imaging, the proposed framework is potentially generalizable to other inverse problems where a well-defined forward model is available. This is an important consideration for assessing the paper's impact on the broader field of computational imaging.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and Well-Organized Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow. The motivation and the approach are clearly explained.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results are compelling and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method.
    *   **Principled Approach:**  The paper provides a principled and theoretically grounded approach to integrating diffusion models with PnP optimization.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Experiments:** The experiments are limited to single-pixel imaging. It would be beneficial to evaluate the performance of the proposed method on other inverse problems, such as super-resolution or deblurring.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper does not explicitly address the computational cost of the proposed method. Diffusion models can be computationally expensive, and the addition of PnP optimization steps may further increase the computational burden. The paper should analyze runtime and compare it to alternatives.
    *   **Parameter Tuning:** The hybrid update introduces a new parameter (δ), and the paper does not provide a detailed analysis of how this parameter should be tuned. Furthermore, the choice of diffusion schedule could have a significant impact on the performance of the proposed method, and the paper does not provide a comprehensive discussion of this aspect.
    *   **Dependence on Quality of the Prior:** The performance strongly relies on a pretrained score model (or a denoiser). The success is heavily dependent on its quality and applicability to the given task. This aspect requires more clarity and discussion.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of computational imaging by providing a novel and effective way to combine generative priors with physical measurement models. The proposed framework could be adopted by other researchers to develop new methods for solving a wide range of inverse problems. However, the limitations need to be addressed to allow its broader adoption.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field. The idea of decoupling DDIM and integrating with PnP optimization, especially the hybrid data consistency component, is novel and well-justified. The experimental results validate the proposed approach and demonstrates improved performance. However, the limitations regarding the computational cost, parameter tuning, and the dependence on the quality of the prior prevent a higher score. While the framework shows strong potential, more extensive experimentation across diverse inverse problems and a deeper analysis of the practical considerations are needed to establish its widespread applicability and significance. The score reflects the meaningful contribution, acknowledging both the novelty and the limitations of the paper.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MetaLLMix : An XAI Aided LLM-Meta-learning Based Approach for Hyper-parameters Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09387v1)
- **Authors**: Mohammed Tiouti, Mohamed Bal-Ghaoui
- **Abstract**: Effective model and hyperparameter selection remains a major challenge in deep learning, often requiring extensive expertise and computation. While AutoML and large language models (LLMs) promise automation, current LLM-based approaches rely on trial and error and expensive APIs, which provide limited interpretability and generalizability. We propose MetaLLMiX, a zero-shot hyperparameter optimization framework combining meta-learning, explainable AI, and efficient LLM reasoning. By leveraging historical experiment outcomes with SHAP explanations, MetaLLMiX recommends optimal hyperparameters and pretrained models without additional trials. We further employ an LLM-as-judge evaluation to control output format, accuracy, and completeness. Experiments on eight medical imaging datasets using nine open-source lightweight LLMs show that MetaLLMiX achieves competitive or superior performance to traditional HPO methods while drastically reducing computational cost. Our local deployment outperforms prior API-based approaches, achieving optimal results on 5 of 8 tasks, response time reductions of 99.6-99.9%, and the fastest training times on 6 datasets (2.4-15.7x faster), maintaining accuracy within 1-5% of best-performing baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MetaLLMiX: An XAI Aided LLM-Meta-Learning Based Approach for Hyper-Parameters Optimization":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MetaLLMiX, a novel framework for hyperparameter optimization (HPO) that combines meta-learning, explainable AI (XAI), and large language models (LLMs). MetaLLMiX aims to address the limitations of traditional HPO methods and existing LLM-based approaches by leveraging historical experiment data enriched with SHAP explanations to recommend optimal hyperparameter configurations and pre-trained model selections in a zero-shot manner.  It uses LLMs for reasoning and decision-making, with SHAP values providing interpretability. The framework also incorporates an LLM-as-judge evaluation to control output quality. The authors evaluate MetaLLMiX on medical image classification tasks, comparing its performance against traditional methods like Random Search (RS) and Bayesian Optimization (BO) using various open-source LLMs. The results show that MetaLLMiX achieves competitive to superior performance with significantly reduced computational cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel combination of techniques. The integration of meta-learning with LLMs for HPO isn't entirely new, but the addition of SHAP-based explanations, and particularly the use of a lightweight LLM for zero-shot HPO with this explainability component, distinguishes this work from existing approaches. Also, the LLM-as-judge mechanism, while simple, is a sensible addition for controlling output quality. The focus on open-source LLMs to make the approach more accessible is also a good direction.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in its potential to make HPO more accessible and efficient, particularly in resource-constrained environments.  The substantial reduction in optimization time (claimed as 99.6-99.9%) is a strong argument for its practical utility. The interpretability aspect is also valuable, as it can build trust in the automated HPO process.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Reduced Computational Cost:** The most compelling strength is the significant reduction in optimization time compared to traditional HPO methods.
    *   **Interpretability:** The use of SHAP values provides a degree of transparency and justification for the recommended hyperparameter configurations.
    *   **Zero-Shot Capability:** The framework doesn't require iterative experimentation, making it suitable for rapid prototyping.
    *   **Use of Open-Source LLMs:**  The emphasis on open-source LLMs lowers the barrier to entry compared to approaches relying solely on proprietary APIs like GPT-4.
    *   **LLM-as-Judge Evaluation:**  The evaluation of the LLM output adds a valuable quality control mechanism.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Meta-Dataset Quality:** The framework's performance is heavily dependent on the quality and diversity of the meta-dataset. The paper acknowledges this limitation, noting that historical experiments generated through random sampling might introduce suboptimal patterns. The meta-dataset construction is a potential bottleneck.
    *   **Domain Specificity:** The evaluation is limited to medical imaging classification tasks, raising questions about generalizability to other domains. The paper does discuss extending it, but the current validation is narrow.
    *   **LLM Variability:** The performance variability across different LLMs highlights a dependency on careful LLM selection.  While the LLM-as-judge helps, a more robust method for LLM selection would improve the framework.
    *   **Limited Model Exploration:** While architecture search is claimed, the base model options used are quite limited during their experiments which may reduce the effectiveness of architecture selection.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** The "judge" LLM scores, while useful, require deeper analysis of potential biases, particularly given the subjectivity involved in assessing factors like fluency and conciseness. A more quantitative method for assessing "explanation quality" could be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:**  MetaLLMiX has the potential to influence the field of automated machine learning by demonstrating the effectiveness of combining meta-learning, explainable AI, and LLMs for HPO. It could spur further research into developing more efficient, interpretable, and accessible HPO methods, particularly for resource-constrained environments. The focus on open-source LLMs could also promote wider adoption of these techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably innovative and practical approach to HPO with some notable strengths, but it also faces limitations regarding its domain-specific evaluation, reliance on the meta-dataset, and LLM selection variability. The computational cost reduction is a compelling argument for its value, but broader validation is needed. The integration of SHAP is interesting, but doesn't break significant new ground in explainability itself.

Therefore, the paper warrants a score of **7**.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### HD-MoE: Hybrid and Dynamic Parallelism for Mixture-of-Expert LLMs with 3D Near-Memory Processing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09420v1)
- **Authors**: Haochen Huang, Shuzhang Zhong, Zhe Zhang, Shuangchen Li, Dimin Niu, Hongzhong Zheng, Runsheng Wang, Meng Li
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) with Mixture-of-Expert (MoE) architectures achieve superior model performance with reduced computation costs, but at the cost of high memory capacity and bandwidth requirements. Near-Memory Processing (NMP) accelerators that stack memory directly on the compute through hybrid bonding have demonstrated high bandwidth with high energy efficiency, becoming a promising architecture for MoE models. However, as NMP accelerators comprise distributed memory and computation, how to map the MoE computation directly determines the LLM inference efficiency. Existing parallel mapping strategies, including Tensor Parallelism (TP) and Expert Parallelism (EP), suffer from either high communication costs or unbalanced computation utilization, leading to inferior efficiency. The dynamic routing mechanism of MoE LLMs further aggravates the efficiency challenges. Therefore, in this paper, we propose HD-MoE to automatically optimize the MoE parallel computation across an NMP accelerator. HD-MoE features an offline automatic hybrid parallel mapping algorithm and an online dynamic scheduling strategy to reduce the communication costs while maximizing the computation utilization. With extensive experimental results, we demonstrate that HD-MoE achieves a speedup ranging from 1.1x to 1.8x over TP, 1.1x to 1.5x over EP, and 1.0x to 1.4x over the baseline Hybrid TP-EP with Compute-Balanced parallelism strategies.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary, a critical evaluation of the novelty and significance, and a justified score for the paper "HD-MoE: Hybrid and Dynamic Parallelism for Mixture-of-Expert LLMs with 3D Near-Memory Processing."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of deploying Mixture-of-Expert (MoE) Large Language Models (LLMs) on 3D Near-Memory Processing (NMP) architectures. It argues that existing parallel mapping strategies like Tensor Parallelism (TP) and Expert Parallelism (EP) are inefficient due to high communication costs or unbalanced computation utilization, especially considering the dynamic routing of MoE LLMs.  The authors propose HD-MoE, a hybrid and dynamic parallelism framework. HD-MoE uses an offline algorithm to combine TP for frequently activated experts and EP for infrequently activated ones, and an online strategy to dynamically schedule experts to maximize computation utilization and reduce communication overhead.  Experimental results demonstrate speedups compared to TP, EP, and a baseline hybrid approach. The code is publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The novelty lies in the combination of several elements:

1.  *Hybrid Parallelism Tailored for 3D NMP:*  While hybrid parallelism combining tensor and expert parallelism isn't entirely new in the context of distributed training (e.g., on GPU clusters), its specific adaptation and optimization for 3D NMP architectures is more novel. The unique constraints of 3D NMP, such as limited memory per bank and the distributed NoC, require a different approach than simply replicating experts like in cloud scenarios.
2.  *Dynamic Scheduling with Prediction:*  The inclusion of dynamic scheduling based on expert activation prediction adds another layer of optimization.  Predicting expert activation has been explored in offloading scenarios, but the integration with a hybrid parallelism strategy in 3D NMP is a key differentiator.
3.  *Automated Optimization via LP:*  The automated optimization using linear programming to determine the optimal hybrid parallelism configuration is a strength. This reduces the need for manual tuning, which can be complex and time-consuming. The analytical modeling is also a plus, although it's always a simplification of the real-world system.

The paper is *incremental* rather than revolutionary. It builds upon existing concepts but combines them in a specific way that's relevant to a particular emerging hardware architecture (3D NMP). However, applying to the unique challenges presented by NMP is a novel area of exploration.

*Significance:*

The paper addresses a relevant and important problem: how to efficiently deploy MoE LLMs on emerging 3D NMP architectures.  MoE models are becoming increasingly popular, and 3D NMP is a promising solution to overcome memory bottlenecks.  Therefore, techniques that improve MoE inference on 3D NMP are valuable. The public availability of the code contributes positively.

The significance is somewhat limited by the relative immaturity of 3D NMP technology. While promising, 3D NMP is not yet widely deployed. Thus, the immediate impact is more likely to be on researchers exploring this architecture rather than on practitioners deploying large-scale models in production.

*Strengths:*

*   Clear problem definition and motivation.
*   Well-defined framework with offline and online optimization.
*   Automated optimization reduces manual tuning.
*   Experimental validation demonstrates performance improvements.
*   Code is publicly available.
*   Good model of memory bandwidth

*Weaknesses:*

*   Incremental novelty rather than a breakthrough.
*   The significance is somewhat limited by the current state of 3D NMP adoption.
*   The analytical model simplifies reality and may not capture all the complexities of the NoC.
*   Lack of comparison with other dynamic approaches

*Potential Influence:*

The paper could influence research in the following ways:

*   It provides a starting point for exploring MoE deployment on 3D NMP.
*   The hybrid parallelism and dynamic scheduling strategies could be adopted and further refined.
*   The automated optimization approach could inspire other researchers to develop similar techniques.
*   The open-source code could facilitate further experimentation and development.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid, incremental contribution to a relevant and growing area. The combination of hybrid parallelism, dynamic scheduling, and automated optimization, specifically tailored for 3D NMP, is a novel aspect. While the significance is currently constrained by the limited deployment of 3D NMP, the paper has the potential to influence future research and development in this area. I do not believe the paper is particularly revolutionary or groundbreaking, which influences the score somewhat.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.PF
- **Score**: 7/10

### ENSI: Efficient Non-Interactive Secure Inference for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09424v1)
- **Authors**: Zhiyu He, Maojiang Wang, Xinwen Gao, Yuchuan Luo, Lin Liu, Shaojing Fu
- **Abstract**: Secure inference enables privacy-preserving machine learning by leveraging cryptographic protocols that support computations on sensitive user data without exposing it. However, integrating cryptographic protocols with large language models (LLMs) presents significant challenges, as the inherent complexity of these protocols, together with LLMs' massive parameter scale and sophisticated architectures, severely limits practical usability. In this work, we propose ENSI, a novel non-interactive secure inference framework for LLMs, based on the principle of co-designing the cryptographic protocols and LLM architecture. ENSI employs an optimized encoding strategy that seamlessly integrates CKKS scheme with a lightweight LLM variant, BitNet, significantly reducing the computational complexity of encrypted matrix multiplications. In response to the prohibitive computational demands of softmax under homomorphic encryption (HE), we pioneer the integration of the sigmoid attention mechanism with HE as a seamless, retraining-free alternative. Furthermore, by embedding the Bootstrapping operation within the RMSNorm process, we efficiently refresh ciphertexts while markedly decreasing the frequency of costly bootstrapping invocations. Experimental evaluations demonstrate that ENSI achieves approximately an 8x acceleration in matrix multiplications and a 2.6x speedup in softmax inference on CPU compared to state-of-the-art method, with the proportion of bootstrapping is reduced to just 1%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ENSI, a new framework for efficient non-interactive secure inference of large language models (LLMs) using homomorphic encryption (HE). ENSI focuses on a co-design approach, optimizing both the LLM architecture (using BitNet) and the cryptographic protocols (RNS-CKKS). Key innovations include: (1) a column-wise encoding strategy integrated with BitNet to reduce computational complexity and avoid ciphertext interleaving; (2) replacing softmax with the sigmoid attention mechanism for HE-friendliness; (3) embedding bootstrapping within the RMSNorm process to minimize bootstrapping frequency. Experiments show ENSI achieves speedups in matrix multiplications and softmax inference compared to existing methods and lowers the required bootstrapping operations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The co-design approach is a strong point. Integrating BitNet's 1-bit weights, the modified encoding, and RMSNorm bootstrapping is a novel combination. The use of sigmoid attention instead of softmax is not entirely novel in HE secure inference (others replace softmax, like PowerFormer), but the specific way it's integrated and the claimed retraining-free nature are noteworthy. The elimination of multiplication in PCMM is also a substantial contribution.

*   **Significance:** Secure inference for LLMs is a critical problem. This work addresses the key bottlenecks: computationally expensive matrix multiplications and activation functions in HE. The claimed speedups and reduction in bootstrapping frequency are practically significant. If the accuracy on larger models is indeed negligibly affected, this can potentially have a large impact on HE-based LLM deployment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The co-design methodology is clearly articulated and justified.
    *   The specific optimizations (modified encoding, sigmoid attention, RMSNorm bootstrapping) are well-explained.
    *   The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating significant performance improvements.
    *   The implementation details and open-source code are positive for reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper states that the secure inference is applied *post-training*, there are no training results to demonstrate the viability of using BitNet with SigmoidAttn. This is an important point, since it is known that the choice of activation functions during training does affect the final model performance. If the accuracy of BitNet is indeed decreased using SigmoidAttn, the authors are essentially comparing apples and oranges.
    *   The accuracy results in Table IV are only presented for BitNet-3B model. However, it would have been better to have compared the performance on LLaMA-3-700M.
    *   More details on the HE parameter selection (N', L, K) and security analysis would strengthen the paper. While the paper states a 128-bit security level, a more detailed justification would be beneficial.
    *   While the paper shows how to achieve 8x performance improvement for matrix multiplication, there is no discussion on the model's overall performance gain with the matrix acceleration applied.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to advance secure LLM inference, especially in resource-constrained environments. The combination of efficient HE techniques with a lightweight LLM architecture makes it more practical to deploy secure LLM services.

*   **Further Considerations:**
    *   The trade-off between accuracy and efficiency for replacing softmax with sigmoid attention needs further investigation, especially on diverse datasets and model sizes.
    *   The scalability of ENSI to even larger LLMs (e.g., billions of parameters) should be explored.
    *   The communication overhead (data transfer) wasn't really the focus, but should be considered when thinking about overall system efficiency.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a compelling and well-executed solution for efficient secure inference of LLMs. The co-design approach, specific optimizations, and experimental results show significant progress. However, the lack of a detailed security analysis, limited accuracy results on models with more parameters, and uncertainty regarding accuracy impact if SigmoidAttn is adopted make a higher score difficult to justify. While the contributions are novel and significant, additional experimentation and analysis are needed to fully validate the practicality and security of ENSI. The paper certainly pushes the state-of-the-art and is likely to stimulate more research in this area.


- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### GrACE: A Generative Approach to Better Confidence Elicitation in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09438v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaohan Zhang, Ziquan Liu, Ioannis Patras
- **Abstract**: Assessing the reliability of Large Language Models (LLMs) by confidence elicitation is a prominent approach to AI safety in high-stakes applications, such as healthcare and finance. Existing methods either require expensive computational overhead or suffer from poor calibration, making them impractical and unreliable for real-world deployment. In this work, we propose GrACE, a Generative Approach to Confidence Elicitation that enables scalable and reliable confidence elicitation for LLMs. GrACE adopts a novel mechanism in which the model expresses confidence by the similarity between the last hidden state and the embedding of a special token appended to the vocabulary, in real-time. We fine-tune the model for calibrating the confidence with calibration targets associated with accuracy. Experiments with three LLMs and two benchmark datasets show that the confidence produced by GrACE achieves the best discriminative capacity and calibration on open-ended generation tasks, outperforming six competing methods without resorting to additional sampling or an auxiliary model. Moreover, we propose two strategies for improving test-time scaling based on confidence induced by GrACE. Experimental results show that using GrACE not only improves the accuracy of the final decision but also significantly reduces the number of required samples in the test-time scaling scheme, indicating the potential of GrACE as a practical solution for deploying LLMs with scalable, reliable, and real-time confidence estimation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GrACE: A Generative Approach to Better Confidence Elicitation in Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GrACE (Generative Approach to Confidence Elicitation), a new method for eliciting more reliable confidence scores from large language models (LLMs).  Unlike existing methods that rely on post-generation evaluation or verbalized confidence, GrACE works *during* generation. It does this by adding a special token `<CNF>` to the LLM's vocabulary. The model is then fine-tuned to express its confidence through the similarity between the hidden state at the time of generating `<CNF>` and the embedding of the `<CNF>` token itself.  A key part of the method is a calibration process that groups training data based on internal model confidence and uses the empirical accuracy of each group as a fine-tuning target. This avoids directly using potentially noisy binary labels. The authors show that GrACE produces better-calibrated and more discriminative confidence scores compared to several existing methods, leading to improved performance in open-ended generation tasks and test-time scaling (TTS). They also demonstrate better generalizability to out-of-domain examples.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of GrACE has clear novelty.
    *   **On-the-fly Confidence:** Generating the confidence score during generation, especially leveraging the existing projection layer for speed, is a smart and practical design choice.
    *   **Calibration Strategy:** Using grouped accuracy as a fine-tuning target is clever way to smooth the confidence target.
    *   **`<CNF>` Token:** Using the dedicated `<CNF>` token to steer the model into a reflective behavior in the latent space is a well-motivated approach.
    *   **Test-Time Scaling:** Integration with TTS by showing performance gains is a good, clear demonstration that GrACE's strengths can translate into better performance in real-world-inspired applications.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem: the unreliable confidence scores of LLMs.  This problem significantly impacts the real-world deployment and trustworthiness of LLMs in high-stakes areas.
    *   **Practicality:** Generating confidences at test time with minimal overhead helps translate this from "interesting research" to "something that can actually be deployed."
    *   **Results:** Quantitative results show clear improvements over several existing methods, especially in calibration metrics, in the zero-shot/out-of-domain setting.
    *   **Test-Time Scaling:** Improving on Self-Consistency is another strong outcome since that's a widely used method for increasing confidence for LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear and Well-Motivated:** The paper is well-written, clearly explaining the method and its motivations. The figures are helpful.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:**  The experimental setup is extensive. It includes multiple models, datasets, and comparisons against a good range of baselines.
    *   **Ablation Study:**  The ablation study provides insights into the contributions of the different components of GrACE.
    *   **TTS Integration:** Demonstrating the benefits of GrACE for test-time scaling strengthens the practicality argument.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on ROUGE:** Using ROUGE score as the sole correctness indicator, while common, is a simplification. More nuanced human evaluation or more sophisticated automated evaluation could further strengthen the claims.  It's also not clear if these findings will generalize from open-ended question answering to all generative tasks.
    *   **Implementation Details:** While there is a short implementation detail section, the paper could benefit from a link to code to ensure reproducibility (although this is common for papers under review).
    *   **Limited scope in Limitations section:** The limitations section addresses relatively narrow problems in GrACE, but could benefit from a broader discussion about the limitations of using confidence estimates in general (e.g. a model that expresses high confidence doesn't *necessarily* have good performance; it's *necessary* but not *sufficient* for better performance.)
    *   **Case Study:** This section could benefit from more extensive analysis.

*   **Potential Influence:**  GrACE could become a standard technique for confidence elicitation in LLMs, particularly in applications where real-time performance is critical.  The method is relatively simple to implement and could be easily adopted by practitioners. The focus on calibrated confidences will become increasingly important for trust.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

GrACE presents a solid and novel approach to confidence elicitation. It offers several compelling advantages over existing methods: on-the-fly confidence generation, an original calibration strategy, and clear improvements in performance and generalizability. The results on both the standalone calibration task and in conjunction with test-time scaling methods provide strong evidence of its effectiveness.

The weaknesses are mostly related to the evaluation strategy (e.g. reliance on ROUGE) and some discussion points in the analysis/limitations, rather than fundamental flaws in the approach itself. These are areas where future work could build upon and further validate the method. Overall, the paper contributes significantly to the field of LLM reliability and has a good chance of influencing future research and development. Therefore, a score of 7.5 is appropriate.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### TORSO: Template-Oriented Reasoning Towards General Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09448v2)
- **Authors**: Minhyuk Kim, Seungyoon Lee, Heuiseok Lim
- **Abstract**: The approaches that guide Large Language Models (LLMs) to emulate human reasoning during response generation have emerged as an effective method for enabling them to solve complex problems in a step-by-step manner, thereby achieving superior performance. However, most existing approaches using few-shot prompts to generate responses heavily depend on the provided examples, limiting the utilization of the model's inherent reasoning capabilities. Moreover, constructing task-specific few-shot prompts is often costly and may lead to inconsistencies across different tasks. In this work, we introduce Template-Oriented Reasoning (TORSO), which elicits the model to utilize internal reasoning abilities to generate proper responses across various tasks without the need for manually crafted few-shot examples. Our experimental results demonstrate that TORSO achieves strong performance on diverse LLMs benchmarks with reasonable rationales.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Template-Oriented Reasoning (TORSO), a novel approach to guide Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate responses using their inherent reasoning abilities, without relying on task-specific few-shot prompts or additional training data.  TORSO works by injecting specific tokens at the beginning and end of the decoding process. The `<reasoning>` token prompts the model to start generating a rationale, and the `</reasoning><answer>` tokens guide it to wrap up the rationale and provide a final answer. The paper evaluates TORSO across various LLM benchmarks, demonstrating its superiority over baseline in-context learning methods like Chain-of-Thought (CoT), Tree-of-Thoughts (ToT), and Least-to-Most (LtM).  The authors also perform qualitative and ablation studies to analyze the quality of generated rationales and the impact of different template configurations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper presents a moderately novel approach. The idea of manipulating the decoding process through token injection isn't entirely new (e.g., using pause tokens). However, TORSO's specific application of this technique to explicitly unlock and structure the reasoning process, rather than just improve inference time, distinguishes it.  The use of templates like `<reasoning>` and `<answer>` to guide the generation is intuitive but effective.

**Significance:**  The significance lies in potentially reducing the reliance on carefully crafted, task-specific prompts and extensive training data for reasoning.  If TORSO can consistently improve performance across diverse tasks and models with minimal intervention, it could streamline the development and deployment of reasoning-enabled LLMs. It provides a simple and computationally efficient way to tap into the LLM's internal knowledge. The study offers an interesting insight that excessive manual interventions (e.g., crafted few-shot prompts) may hinder the internal coherence of LLMs reasoning.

**Strengths:**

*   **Simplicity and Efficiency:** TORSO is conceptually simple and computationally lightweight.  It doesn't require training or complex architectures.
*   **Broad Applicability:**  The results demonstrate improvements across various tasks and models.
*   **Qualitative Analysis:**  The inclusion of qualitative evaluation using GPT-4o judging model and examples to show the plausibility of generated rationales is valuable.
*   **Ablation Study:** The template ablation study provides insights into the importance of the chosen template structure.
* **Strong experimental results:** The experimental results appear to robustly support the claim of effectiveness.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The paper acknowledges limitations regarding out-of-distribution reasoning. TORSO likely relies on reasoning patterns implicitly learned during pre-training, making it less effective for completely novel scenarios.
*   **Template Dependence:** While the ablation study explores template variations, there's still a dependence on the chosen template.  The optimal template might vary across different models or tasks. This is a weakness of the paper as it indicates that the template needs to be carefully selected to be effective.
*   **Lack of theoretical analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth theoretical analysis of why TORSO is effective, providing a stronger explanation for the observed results. Why do the specific tokens work in terms of LLM's architecture and/or training data?
*   **Comparison Model:** The models used for comparison (e.g., Mistral-7B-Instruct) are smaller models. Using bigger models might be helpful for better results
*   **Qualitative evaluation metrics:** Using models like GPT-4 to assess the qualitative aspect can be subjective

**Potential Influence:**

TORSO could influence future research in a few ways:

*   **Prompt Engineering:** It encourages a shift from crafting explicit reasoning chains to a more implicit guidance approach.
*   **Model Adaptation:**  It highlights the potential for adapting pre-trained models without extensive fine-tuning by carefully controlling the decoding process.
*   **Reasoning Frameworks:** It could serve as a building block for more sophisticated reasoning frameworks that combine template-based guidance with other techniques.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of **7**.  The paper is well-written, presents a relatively novel approach, and demonstrates empirical evidence of its effectiveness. The significance of the work is moderate. It offers a practical technique for improving reasoning in LLMs with minimal overhead. However, the paper's limitations regarding out-of-distribution scenarios, template dependence, and the need for a more thorough theoretical analysis prevent it from achieving a higher score. While valuable, the research has limited transformative impact on the field.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Composable Score-based Graph Diffusion Model for Multi-Conditional Molecular Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09451v1)
- **Authors**: Anjie Qiao, Zhen Wang, Chuan Chen, DeFu Lian, Enhong Chen
- **Abstract**: Controllable molecular graph generation is essential for material and drug discovery, where generated molecules must satisfy diverse property constraints. While recent advances in graph diffusion models have improved generation quality, their effectiveness in multi-conditional settings remains limited due to reliance on joint conditioning or continuous relaxations that compromise fidelity. To address these limitations, we propose Composable Score-based Graph Diffusion model (CSGD), the first model that extends score matching to discrete graphs via concrete scores, enabling flexible and principled manipulation of conditional guidance. Building on this foundation, we introduce two score-based techniques: Composable Guidance (CoG), which allows fine-grained control over arbitrary subsets of conditions during sampling, and Probability Calibration (PC), which adjusts estimated transition probabilities to mitigate train-test mismatches. Empirical results on four molecular datasets show that CSGD achieves state-of-the-art performance, with a 15.3% average improvement in controllability over prior methods, while maintaining high validity and distributional fidelity. Our findings highlight the practical advantages of score-based modeling for discrete graph generation and its capacity for flexible, multi-property molecular design.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Composable Score-based Graph Diffusion (CSGD), a novel approach for multi-conditional molecular generation. CSGD extends score matching to discrete graphs through concrete scores, enabling flexible manipulation of conditional guidance. It proposes two key techniques: Composable Guidance (CoG), which allows fine-grained control over subsets of conditions, and Probability Calibration (PC), which mitigates train-test mismatches by adjusting transition probabilities. Experiments on four molecular datasets demonstrate that CSGD achieves state-of-the-art performance in controllability while maintaining high validity and distributional fidelity, suggesting practical advantages for multi-property molecular design.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in extending score matching to discrete graphs in the context of molecular generation via concrete scores. While score-based diffusion models are well-established in other domains (especially continuous ones like images), their application to discrete graph generation, with a focus on composable conditional guidance, is a significant contribution. The CoG and PC mechanisms are also novel in their application to molecular graph generation, offering specific techniques for improving controllability and fidelity. Prior work in this area often used joint conditioning or continuous relaxations, which have limitations that CSGD addresses. Thus, the novelty is primarily in adapting and extending existing score-based methods to a new domain and problem.

* **Significance:**  Controllable molecular generation is a critical task in drug discovery and materials science. The paper demonstrates a substantial improvement in controllability (15.3% average improvement) over existing methods, making CSGD a potentially valuable tool for researchers in these fields. The ability to flexibly control different properties and generate molecules that satisfy complex constraints can accelerate the design process. The improvement in validity and distribution fidelity ensures that generated molecules are not only controllable but also chemically realistic and diverse.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides extensive experimental results across multiple datasets, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance.
    * **Clear and Well-Defined Techniques:**  The CoG and PC methods are clearly explained and easy to understand.
    * **Addresses a Practical Problem:** The problem of multi-conditional molecular generation is of high practical relevance.
    * **Solid Implementation:** The paper builds upon and adapts existing architectures, leveraging proven methods.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Independence Assumption:** The CoG method relies on the conditional independence assumption of properties, which may not always hold in real-world scenarios. The paper acknowledges this limitation and suggests that handling correlated conditions is a direction for future research.
    * **Complexity:** While the techniques are clearly explained, the overall model involves multiple components (concrete scores, CoG, PC), which may increase the complexity of implementation.
    * **Incremental Advance:** While technically solid and demonstrating clear improvement, the advances could be viewed as incremental. It builds directly on existing techniques such as SEDD and GraphDiT, although it extends and adapts them in clever ways.

* **Potential Impact:**  CSGD has the potential to significantly impact molecular design and discovery by providing a more controllable and efficient way to generate molecules with desired properties. If the code and models are readily available, the ease of use will have a large effect on its uptake. The score-based techniques it introduces could also inspire further research in applying score-based models to other discrete graph generation tasks. The flexible design aspect of being able to add or remove constraints on-the-fly can also have a wide impact on molecular design.

* **Score Justification:** Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, the paper merits a score of **7**. It presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of molecular generation, with strong empirical evidence supporting its effectiveness. However, the reliance on the independence assumption, the complexity of the model, and the incremental nature of the advance limit its overall impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### FlexiD-Fuse: Flexible number of inputs multi-modal medical image fusion based on diffusion model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09456v1)
- **Authors**: Yushen Xu, Xiaosong Li, Yuchun Wang, Xiaoqi Cheng, Huafeng Li, Haishu Tan
- **Abstract**: Different modalities of medical images provide unique physiological and anatomical information for diseases. Multi-modal medical image fusion integrates useful information from different complementary medical images with different modalities, producing a fused image that comprehensively and objectively reflects lesion characteristics to assist doctors in clinical diagnosis. However, existing fusion methods can only handle a fixed number of modality inputs, such as accepting only two-modal or tri-modal inputs, and cannot directly process varying input quantities, which hinders their application in clinical settings. To tackle this issue, we introduce FlexiD-Fuse, a diffusion-based image fusion network designed to accommodate flexible quantities of input modalities. It can end-to-end process two-modal and tri-modal medical image fusion under the same weight. FlexiD-Fuse transforms the diffusion fusion problem, which supports only fixed-condition inputs, into a maximum likelihood estimation problem based on the diffusion process and hierarchical Bayesian modeling. By incorporating the Expectation-Maximization algorithm into the diffusion sampling iteration process, FlexiD-Fuse can generate high-quality fused images with cross-modal information from source images, independently of the number of input images. We compared the latest two and tri-modal medical image fusion methods, tested them on Harvard datasets, and evaluated them using nine popular metrics. The experimental results show that our method achieves the best performance in medical image fusion with varying inputs. Meanwhile, we conducted extensive extension experiments on infrared-visible, multi-exposure, and multi-focus image fusion tasks with arbitrary numbers, and compared them with the perspective SOTA methods. The results of the extension experiments consistently demonstrate the effectiveness and superiority of our method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FlexiD-Fuse, a novel diffusion-based image fusion network designed to handle a flexible number of input modalities in medical image fusion.  Unlike existing methods that are limited to fixed numbers of input modalities (e.g., two or three), FlexiD-Fuse leverages a hierarchical Bayesian model and expectation-maximization (EM) algorithm within the diffusion sampling process to effectively fuse images from varying input quantities.  It also introduces an ultra-lightweight diffusion fusion Mamba (DFM) model to accelerate inference. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of FlexiD-Fuse through extensive experiments on two-modal and tri-modal medical image fusion tasks, as well as extension experiments on infrared and visible image fusion, multi-exposure image fusion, and multi-focus image fusion.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a Significant Limitation:** The paper tackles a crucial limitation of existing medical image fusion methods: the inability to handle a flexible number of input modalities.  This is a practical concern in clinical settings where the optimal set of modalities varies between patients and diagnostic needs.
*   **Novel Approach:** The use of a diffusion model combined with hierarchical Bayesian modeling and EM is a novel approach to medical image fusion. It allows for the fusion process to adapt to the number of modalities and extract cross-modal information effectively. The transformation of the fixed-input diffusion fusion problem into a maximum likelihood estimation problem is a significant contribution.
*   **Ultra-lightweight DFM:** The introduction of the DFM based on Mamba is a positive development addressing the known computational costs and slow fusion times associated with diffusion models.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:**  The paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments, including comparisons with numerous state-of-the-art methods and a wide range of evaluation metrics. The experiments are performed on medical and non-medical datasets, providing a robust validation of the approach's generalizability.
*   **Performance:** The results demonstrate superior performance in both visual quality and quantitative metrics, exceeding the results of 15 SOTA methods.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost Concerns (mitigated but present):** While the DFM helps with reducing computational cost, diffusion models are inherently more computationally expensive than some encoder-decoder architectures. The paper needs to explicitly quantify the reduction in computation achieved by DFM in comparison to a standard U-Net and discuss the remaining inference times, and memory requirements in the context of practical deployment.
*   **Reliance on Hyperparameters and Specific Settings:** The success of diffusion models is often sensitive to the choice of hyperparameters and settings (diffusion steps, noise schedule, etc.). The paper should further elaborate on the sensitivity analysis to highlight optimal settings.
*   **Limited Multi-Modal Medical Datasets:** The use of the Harvard Medical Image Dataset is appropriate, but the paper could benefit from further experiments on other datasets to show generalizability across multiple different medical institutions and populations.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies are good and do show impact, but are not as comprehensive as they could be in terms of architecture.
*  **Justification for Choices:** While the method seems strong, some choices of algorithm components are not sufficiently justified in terms of why they are appropriate for medical image fusion specifically.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper has a reasonable amount of novelty by addressing the inflexibility limitation and successfully introducing new architectural elements. The practical impact of a fusion method that can handle varying input modality numbers is also valuable. The use of Bayesian methods to augment the diffusion process is reasonably novel. However, the overall combination is incremental and, while effective, follows a trend of improving diffusion-based methods for a particular task.

**Potential Influence:**

FlexiD-Fuse has the potential to influence medical image fusion research by:

*   Encouraging the development of more flexible and adaptable fusion methods.
*   Demonstrating the effectiveness of diffusion models in medical image fusion, especially when combined with Bayesian techniques.
*   Motivating further research into lightweight diffusion models for real-time or resource-constrained applications.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

FlexiD-Fuse addresses a pertinent limitation in medical image fusion and proposes a novel solution based on diffusion models, Bayesian methods, and a Mamba architecture. The experimental results are strong, demonstrating superior performance. The weaknesses identified (mainly regarding computational cost, limited datasets, and sensitivity to hyperparameter settings) slightly reduce the score.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Changing the Paradigm from Dynamic Queries to LLM-generated SQL Queries with Human Intervention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09461v1)
- **Authors**: Ambre Assor, Hyeon Jeon, Sungbok Shin, Jean-Daniel Fekete
- **Abstract**: We propose leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) as an interaction layer for medical visualization systems. In domains like healthcare, where users must navigate high-dimensional, coded, and heterogeneous datasets, LLM-generated queries enable expert medical users to express complex analytical intents in natural language. These intents are then translated into editable and executable queries, replacing the dynamic query interfaces used by traditional visualization systems built around sliders, check boxes, and drop-downs. This interaction model reduces visual clutter and eliminates the need for users to memorize field names or system codes, supporting fluid exploration, with the drawback of not exposing all the filtering criteria. We also reintroduce dynamic queries on demand to better support interactive exploration. We posit that medical users are trained to know the possible filtering options but challenged to remember the details of the attribute names and code values. We demonstrate this paradigm in ParcoursVis, our scalable EventFlow-inspired patient care pathway visualization system powered by the French National Health Data System, one of the largest health data repositories in the world.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Changing the Paradigm from Dynamic Queries to LLM-generated SQL Queries with Human Intervention":

**Summary:**

This paper proposes a shift in how users interact with medical visualization systems, specifically when dealing with Electronic Health Record (EHR) data.  Instead of relying solely on traditional dynamic query interfaces (sliders, checkboxes, etc.), the authors suggest leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to translate natural language queries into SQL queries. This allows medical experts to express complex analytical intents more intuitively. The generated SQL queries are editable and paired with natural language explanations to enhance transparency and user agency. The authors demonstrate the concept in Parcours Vis, a system for visualizing patient care pathways using French National Health Data. They also acknowledge challenges related to dataset standardization and propose solutions like adopting the OMOP data model or creating simplified, flat datasets. Finally, they suggest reviving aspects of dynamic queries by extracting SQL subtrees to generate context-aware widgets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs for query generation in EHR visualization systems isn't entirely novel.  Several papers already explore LLMs for database interaction (cited in the paper itself).  The primary novelty lies in the focus on human intervention (editable SQL and paired explanations) and the specific context of large, complex EHR datasets like the French SNDS. The proposal to combine this approach with elements of traditional dynamic queries through SQL subtree extraction adds a layer of incremental novelty.

* **Significance:** The significance is tied to the practical problem the paper addresses: the difficulty of navigating and querying large, complex EHR databases. Traditional dynamic query interfaces become unwieldy with a large number of variables.  LLMs offer a potentially more scalable and user-friendly alternative for expert users.  The emphasis on transparency and editability is crucial in the medical domain, where trust and accountability are paramount. However, the lack of a rigorous user study to validate the benefits and usability of this approach weakens the paper's significance.  While the initial demonstration with the Mistral LLM is promising, a real-world evaluation with medical professionals is needed to determine if this paradigm shift is truly effective and safe. The suggested solutions for dataset standardization, although not revolutionary, highlight important practical considerations for deploying LLMs in healthcare.

* **Strengths:**
    * Addresses a real-world problem of querying complex medical data.
    * Proposes a concrete and implementable solution using LLMs.
    * Highlights the importance of human intervention and transparency in LLM-based systems for healthcare.
    * Identifies and proposes solutions for dataset standardization challenges.
    * Suggests a useful bridge back to dynamic queries.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Lacks a thorough user study to validate the usability and effectiveness of the proposed approach.  It's a conceptual paper.
    * Limited integration of LLMs in the current system -- focused on a single step for initial investigation.
    * The novelty is incremental rather than groundbreaking. Similar ideas exist.
    * The evaluation is very preliminary.

* **Potential Impact:** If validated by future research, this approach could significantly improve the accessibility and usability of large EHR databases for medical experts, leading to better insights and decision-making.  The focus on transparency and user agency is particularly important in the context of AI in healthcare, where ethical considerations are paramount.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable idea with potential for real-world impact, but it lacks empirical validation.  The core concepts are built on existing research, making the novelty incremental. The strength of the work lies in adapting the idea to the specific challenges of EHR data and advocating for crucial design principles (transparency, editability) when applying LLMs in healthcare. The current form doesn't fully show the benefits or the potential risks.

Score: 6

(Rationale:  A score of 6 reflects a good idea with practical relevance but acknowledges the incremental novelty, lack of empirical validation, and very preliminary demonstration. It's a promising direction, but the potential impact remains uncertain until further research is conducted.)

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### Database Views as Explanations for Relational Deep Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09482v1)
- **Authors**: Agapi Rissaki, Ilias Fountalis, Wolfgang Gatterbauer, Benny Kimelfeld
- **Abstract**: In recent years, there has been significant progress in the development of deep learning models over relational databases, including architectures based on heterogeneous graph neural networks (hetero-GNNs) and heterogeneous graph transformers. In effect, such architectures state how the database records and links (e.g., foreign-key references) translate into a large, complex numerical expression, involving numerous learnable parameters. This complexity makes it hard to explain, in human-understandable terms, how a model uses the available data to arrive at a given prediction. We present a novel framework for explaining machine-learning models over relational databases, where explanations are view definitions that highlight focused parts of the database that mostly contribute to the model's prediction. We establish such global abductive explanations by adapting the classic notion of determinacy by Nash, Segoufin, and Vianu (2010). In addition to tuning the tradeoff between determinacy and conciseness, the framework allows controlling the level of granularity by adopting different fragments of view definitions, such as ones highlighting whole columns, foreign keys between tables, relevant groups of tuples, and so on. We investigate the realization of the framework in the case of hetero-GNNs. We develop heuristic algorithms that avoid the exhaustive search over the space of all databases. We propose techniques that are model-agnostic, and others that are tailored to hetero-GNNs via the notion of learnable masking. Our approach is evaluated through an extensive empirical study on the RelBench collection, covering a variety of domains and different record-level tasks. The results demonstrate the usefulness of the proposed explanations, as well as the efficiency of their generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Database Views as Explanations for Relational Deep Learning":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of explaining predictions made by deep learning models trained on relational databases.  It proposes a novel framework where explanations are defined as SQL views that highlight the most influential parts of the database for a given prediction. The core idea is to adapt the concept of determinacy to a soft and statistical setting: an explanation view is good if the model's prediction is relatively stable (i.e., soft determinacy) even when the rest of the database is perturbed, as long as the explanation view itself remains unchanged. The framework allows for tuning the trade-off between the accuracy and conciseness of the explanations, and supports different fragments of SQL for defining the explanation views. The authors focus on heterogeneous graph neural networks (hetero-GNNs) and develop heuristic algorithms that avoid an exhaustive search of the database space. The algorithms are realized through masking learned variations of the GNN itself. Finally, the framework is evaluated empirically using the RelBench collection, demonstrating the usefulness and efficiency of the explanations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel framework for explaining relational deep learning models. The central idea of using database views as explanations, grounding the interpretations in a formalism familiar to database users, is a significant step. Adapting determinacy to a soft and statistical setting to suit the imperfect nature of machine learning models is a clever theoretical contribution. The mask-learning heuristic for efficient discovery of explanation views in the context of hetero-GNNs also demonstrates novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in the application of deep learning to relational data: explainability.
