### Color Me Correctly: Bridging Perceptual Color Spaces and Text Embeddings for Improved Diffusion Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.10058v1)
- **Authors**: Sung-Lin Tsai, Bo-Lun Huang, Yu Ting Shen, Cheng Yu Yeo, Chiang Tseng, Bo-Kai Ruan, Wen-Sheng Lien, Hong-Han Shuai
- **Abstract**: Accurate color alignment in text-to-image (T2I) generation is critical for applications such as fashion, product visualization, and interior design, yet current diffusion models struggle with nuanced and compound color terms (e.g., Tiffany blue, lime green, hot pink), often producing images that are misaligned with human intent. Existing approaches rely on cross-attention manipulation, reference images, or fine-tuning but fail to systematically resolve ambiguous color descriptions. To precisely render colors under prompt ambiguity, we propose a training-free framework that enhances color fidelity by leveraging a large language model (LLM) to disambiguate color-related prompts and guiding color blending operations directly in the text embedding space. Our method first employs a large language model (LLM) to resolve ambiguous color terms in the text prompt, and then refines the text embeddings based on the spatial relationships of the resulting color terms in the CIELAB color space. Unlike prior methods, our approach improves color accuracy without requiring additional training or external reference images. Experimental results demonstrate that our framework improves color alignment without compromising image quality, bridging the gap between text semantics and visual generation.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Color Me Correctly: Bridging Perceptual Color Spaces and Text Embeddings for Improved Diffusion Generation" addresses the challenge of achieving accurate color representation in text-to-image generation, particularly for nuanced color terms like "Tiffany blue" or "lime green." Current diffusion models often fail in this area due to ambiguous color descriptions. The authors propose a novel framework that uses a large language model (LLM) to disambiguate color terms within prompts and adjusts text embeddings according to their spatial relationships in the CIELAB color space. Importantly, their method does not require additional training or reference images, yet it enhances color fidelity while maintaining image quality. Experimental results confirm that their framework effectively improves color alignment in generated images. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents several notable strengths, contributing to the literature on text-to-image generation: 1. **Novelty**: The integration of a large language model to resolve color ambiguities in text prompts is a fresh approach within the context of diffusion models. The authors circumvent the need for training or external references, which is a considerable advancement over existing techniques that often rely on these factors. 2. **Clarity and Methodology**: The methodology is clearly articulated, and the paper outlines the processes of ambiguation and color refinement effectively. The systematic approach to derive color relationships using CIELAB space is commendable and well-founded in perceptual color science. 3. **Empirical Validation**: The authors provide experimental results that showcase improved color fidelity, enhancing the paper's credibility and practical applicability. However, the extent of experiments and the range of conditions under which the method performs were not detailed; this may limit the generalizability of findings. Despite these strengths, there are notable weaknesses: 1. **Limited Scope of Application**: While the focus on color fidelity is undoubtedly important, the paper primarily addresses a specific limitation in T2I models. The impact on broader multimodal tasks or its adoption in various creative applications may not be substantial. 2. **Potential Over-reliance on LLMs**: The study's effectiveness heavily hinges on the capabilities of large language models, which may exhibit variability based on the specific model utilized or the prompt structure. 3. **Comparative Analysis**: While the paper claims to outperform existing methods, detailed comparative analyses with a wider taxonomy of color handling models would strengthen the work, delineating exactly where its advantages lie. In conclusion, while the paper provides a significant advancement in color alignment for T2I generation with a unique, training-free methodology, its impact may be limited to this niche application. Therefore, I would rate this paper a **7 out of 10**. It presents a valuable contribution with potential for further exploration, yet broader empirical validation and a wider investigation of applications would enhance its significance in the field. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multimodal Mathematical Reasoning Embedded in Aerial Vehicle Imagery: Benchmarking, Analysis, and Exploration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.10059v1)
- **Authors**: Yue Zhou, Litong Feng, Mengcheng Lan, Xue Yang, Qingyun Li, Yiping Ke, Xue Jiang, Wayne Zhang
- **Abstract**: Mathematical reasoning is critical for tasks such as precise distance and area computations, trajectory estimations, and spatial analysis in unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) based remote sensing, yet current vision-language models (VLMs) have not been adequately tested in this domain. To address this gap, we introduce AVI-Math, the first benchmark to rigorously evaluate multimodal mathematical reasoning in aerial vehicle imagery, moving beyond simple counting tasks to include domain-specific knowledge in areas such as geometry, logic, and algebra. The dataset comprises 3,773 high-quality vehicle-related questions captured from UAV views, covering 6 mathematical subjects and 20 topics. The data, collected at varying altitudes and from multiple UAV angles, reflects real-world UAV scenarios, ensuring the diversity and complexity of the constructed mathematical problems. In this paper, we benchmark 14 prominent VLMs through a comprehensive evaluation and demonstrate that, despite their success on previous multimodal benchmarks, these models struggle with the reasoning tasks in AVI-Math. Our detailed analysis highlights significant limitations in the mathematical reasoning capabilities of current VLMs and suggests avenues for future research. Furthermore, we explore the use of Chain-of-Thought prompting and fine-tuning techniques, which show promise in addressing the reasoning challenges in AVI-Math. Our findings not only expose the limitations of VLMs in mathematical reasoning but also offer valuable insights for advancing UAV-based trustworthy VLMs in real-world applications. The code, and datasets will be released at https://github.com/VisionXLab/avi-math
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AVI-MATH, a new benchmark dataset designed to evaluate the mathematical reasoning capabilities of Vision-Language Models (VLMs) in the context of aerial vehicle imagery (AVI). Unlike existing VQA datasets for remote sensing, AVI-MATH focuses on more complex mathematical reasoning beyond simple counting, encompassing geometry, logic, statistics, arithmetic, counting, and algebra. The dataset comprises high-resolution UAV images with detailed metadata about camera parameters and vehicle attributes (brand, model, price, etc.). The authors benchmark 14 prominent VLMs on AVI-MATH and find that they struggle with the dataset's reasoning tasks despite their success on previous multimodal benchmarks. The paper also explores the use of Chain-of-Thought prompting and instruction fine-tuning using AVI-MATH-215K, a newly created instruction dataset to improve VLM performance on the benchmark. The analysis reveals that VLMs have limitations in visual perception in aerial imagery and lack domain-specific knowledge necessary for solving complex reasoning problems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper demonstrates good novelty in several aspects:

*   **New Dataset:** The introduction of AVI-MATH addresses a clear gap in the evaluation of VLMs for remote sensing. Existing RS VQA datasets lack complex mathematical reasoning tasks and fine-grained annotations. AVI-MATH, with its detailed metadata and focus on mathematical reasoning relevant to UAV applications, fills this void.
*   **AVI-MATH-215K for Instruction Tuning:** Creating an instruction tuning dataset specifically tailored for AVI-MATH and its domain is another valuable contribution that enhances the paper's utility.
*   **Domain-Specific Focus:** Applying and adapting VLM evaluation to a specific domain (aerial imagery and UAVs) is novel. The analysis explicitly focuses on the challenges posed by RS imagery.

**Significance:**

*   **Benchmarking VLMs:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of several state-of-the-art VLMs on the new dataset. The results highlight limitations in current VLMs, suggesting the benchmark can differentiate more effectively than existing benchmarks.
*   **In-Depth Analysis:** The paper delivers a detailed analysis of the errors made by VLMs, pinpointing areas where they struggle. This offers valuable insights for improving VLMs for RS applications. The analysis of the effects of resolution, reasoning steps, and the transferability of natural-image knowledge provides substantial contributions.
*   **Practical Relevance:** The work has practical implications for the development of trustworthy autonomous UAV systems that require reliable and interpretable reasoning.

**Strengths:**

*   The paper is well-written and clearly structured.
*   The dataset and experimental setup are well-documented.
*   The analysis is thorough and insightful.
*   The paper explores relevant techniques for improving VLM performance.
*   Addresses a significant gap in existing benchmarks.
*   Focuses on a domain (aerial vehicle imagery) with potential practical applications.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Exploration of Solutions:**  While the paper explores chain of thought prompting and fine-tuning, the exploration remains preliminary.  Deeper dives into prompting methods or alternative model architectures may give greater impact. The experimental setup is minimal, where one epoch of training with specific hyperparameters is used.
*   **Generalization:** Focuses on the Vehicle. Extending the dataset/analysis to other RS domains (e.g., crop monitoring) could broaden the impact.
*   **Chain-of-Thought Limitations:**  The paper readily acknowledges that Chain-of-Thought strategies are ineffective in some scenarios.

**Potential Influence:**

The AVI-MATH dataset has the potential to become a widely used benchmark for evaluating VLMs in the RS community. The analysis and insights presented in the paper will guide future research towards developing more robust and reliable VLMs for UAV-based applications. The 215k instruction dataset could prove to be a key enabler for more domain-specific and accurate VLMs.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by introducing a novel and challenging benchmark dataset for evaluating the mathematical reasoning capabilities of VLMs in the context of aerial vehicle imagery. The thorough analysis of existing VLMs and the exploration of methods to improve their performance offer valuable insights for future research. However, the relatively limited exploration of solutions and the focus solely on vehicles somewhat limit the paper's impact. Also, the lack of comparison with other datasets that provide some form of reasoning (albeit simple) reduces the overall impact. Hence, I believe a balanced score is appropriate.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Established Psychometric vs. Ecologically Valid Questionnaires: Rethinking Psychological Assessments in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.10078v1)
- **Authors**: Dongmin Choi, Woojung Song, Jongwook Han, Eun-Ju Lee, Yohan Jo
- **Abstract**: Researchers have applied established psychometric questionnaires (e.g., BFI, PVQ) to measure the personality traits and values reflected in the responses of Large Language Models (LLMs). However, concerns have been raised about applying these human-designed questionnaires to LLMs. One such concern is their lack of ecological validity--the extent to which survey questions adequately reflect and resemble real-world contexts in which LLMs generate texts in response to user queries. However, it remains unclear how established questionnaires and ecologically valid questionnaires differ in their outcomes, and what insights these differences may provide. In this paper, we conduct a comprehensive comparative analysis of the two types of questionnaires. Our analysis reveals that established questionnaires (1) yield substantially different profiles of LLMs from ecologically valid ones, deviating from the psychological characteristics expressed in the context of user queries, (2) suffer from insufficient items for stable measurement, (3) create misleading impressions that LLMs possess stable constructs, and (4) yield exaggerated profiles for persona-prompted LLMs. Overall, our work cautions against the use of established psychological questionnaires for LLMs. Our code will be released upon publication.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Established Psychometric vs. Ecologically Valid Questionnaires: Rethinking Psychological Assessments in Large Language Models" investigates the limitations of using established psychometric questionnaires (e.g., BFI, PVQ) to assess personality traits and values in LLMs. The authors argue that these questionnaires, designed for humans, lack ecological validity when applied to LLMs, as they don't reflect the real-world conversational context in which LLMs operate.  They compare the results of established questionnaires with those from Value Portrait (VP), a more ecologically valid questionnaire developed using real-world user queries and responses, finding significant discrepancies. The paper identifies several risks associated with relying solely on established questionnaires: different profiles, measurement instability, false impressions of consistent constructs, and exaggerated profiles for persona-prompted LLMs. The study involves a comprehensive analysis of 10 LLMs using both types of questionnaires.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes a significant and timely contribution to the field of LLM evaluation. While the application of psychometric tools to LLMs is growing, this work critically examines the underlying assumptions and potential pitfalls of directly transferring human-centric assessment methods.  The core argument, focusing on the lack of ecological validity, is compelling and well-supported by empirical evidence.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined Problem:** Clearly articulates the issue of using human-designed questionnaires for LLMs.
*   **Solid Methodology:** Employs a rigorous comparative analysis between established questionnaires and a more ecologically valid alternative. The use of multiple models and a bootstrap methodology strengthens the robustness of the findings.
*   **Empirical Evidence:** Provides strong empirical support for the claims. The analysis of Mean Absolute Difference (MAD), Spearman correlations, confidence intervals, and inter-item correlations offers a multi-faceted perspective.
*   **Actionable Insights:** Provides concrete observations and cautions for researchers using psychometric tools to evaluate LLMs. The specific findings related to measurement stability and persona prompting bias are particularly valuable.
*   **Novelty:** The explicit comparison between established and ecologically valid questionnaires and the detailed analysis of discrepancies is a novel contribution. The specific insights into how LLMs respond to reverse-coded questions and the potential for overestimating persona differentiation add further value.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Constructs:** Focuses primarily on Schwartz values and Big Five personality traits. Exploring other psychological constructs could broaden the impact.
*   **Reliance on a Single Ecologically Valid Questionnaire:** While Value Portrait is a valuable contribution, the findings' generalizability could be enhanced by comparison with other ecologically valid assessment methods (if available or by designing an additional one).
*   **Lack of Qualitative Analysis:**  While the quantitative analysis is thorough, incorporating some qualitative analysis of LLM responses (e.g., error analysis) might provide deeper insights into the observed discrepancies.
*   **Generalizability of VP Items:** The ecological validity of VP is linked to the dataset from which its items were derived. The real-world representativeness of that dataset could be further discussed.
*   **Limited languages:** Focused solely on English.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its ability to provide critical guidance to researchers in the field. As LLMs become more sophisticated and are deployed in increasingly complex applications, accurate and reliable evaluation methods are crucial.  This work demonstrates that directly applying human-centric tools can be misleading and highlights the need for developing evaluation methods that are tailored to the unique characteristics of LLMs and their interaction contexts. The work potentially opens avenues for developing LLM-specific evaluation techniques that have both ecological and construct validity.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, the paper represents a substantial and novel contribution, but not a *transformative* one. It is clearly *above average*, but some limitations restrict it from reaching a very high impact score. The paper’s main limitations are: the scope of the paper to only a few well-known constructs, the limited scope of languages evaluated, the reliance on a single ecologically valid metric and the lack of further qualitative analysis that could augment the quantitative analysis.
